| Exact claim audited | For the canonical contact-minimal then actual-diameter-pair-minimal triangle-free global source with `D>=9`, `C=C_*`, `H=H_*`, let `V` be the positive-contact core, `Z` its contact isolates, and `F=conv(V)`. If `R` is deleted and `Y` replaces it with `|Y|=|R|`, the proposed `A'=(A\R) union Y` is unit-separated, every pair involving `Y` has distance `<D`, and `Y` lies in the complement of `int(F)`, then no packet exists if `R` deletes an old unit contact or intersects every old actual diameter pair. `Y` may touch `partial(F)` and `A'` need not be triangle-free. |
| Independent reconstruction | Pin `V\R`; allow `Y union (Z\R)` to move. Every free-variable contact in `A'` is new and incident to `Y`. The union of nonnegative row-stress supports has positive stress on every supported edge. At each free vertex, stress equilibrium and the finite maximum principle place it in the convex hull of pinned vertices, hence in `F`. A supported `Y` endpoint must therefore lie on `partial(F)`; its supporting face forces every supported neighbor onto a hull side, where unit separation and equilibrium give a straight unit chain. Perpendicular free-vertex velocities put each supported edge's equal-velocity subspace properly inside the supported-row kernel. Farkas separation supplies a direction strictly opening every unsupported row; perturbing in that kernel opens the supported rows at second order. One sufficiently small straight motion preserves every inactive lower pair and strict upper pair, opens every new contact, and leaves exactly the old contact graph induced on `A\R`. The two deletion hypotheses then contradict contact minimality or globality. |
| Dependencies and scope | Uses canonical `C-ZRF` for the positive-contact core convention, `C-LOADED-HULL-CAP-BUDGET` for `V subset F`, `C-GENERIC-ISOLATE-ANCHOR-AVOIDANCE` for strict upper/lower margins of retained contact isolates, and `C-CONTACT-BUDGET-DIAMETER-EXCHANGE-ENDPOINT` for the final finite minimality contradiction. All source labels remain accounted for. No Delaunay edges, KKT stress of the original source, PSD claim, internal-wall capacity, moved-core construction, or triangle-free temporary packet is used. |
| Counterexamples attempted | Checked packets strictly outside the hull and touching its boundary; exposed vertices versus side-interior points; unpinned stress components; multiple chains meeting at pins; retained isolates on/off the hull; new contacts among packet points or between packets and retained isolates; initial unit triangles; the last-diameter-pair deletion case; and all inactive lower/upper margins. No counterexample survived the stated strict hypotheses. |
| Verdict and canonical disposition | PASS — promote `C-EXTERIOR-STATIC-PACKET-BARRIER`. Mark `C-EXTERIOR-ONE-SITE-SATURATION` superseded as a direct special case; its independent Audit.109 remains historical provenance. |

It produces no packet or interior capacity and does not move the retained positive-contact core. The full-source moving-core/payment endpoint and C-II/Level I remain open. |

| Exact claim audited | For a finite unit-separated source of diameter D, if a,b have ell<D, their actual diameter partners lie in the same closed side of ab, and a source point lies strictly opposite, then (1+D^-2)ell^2+2ell/D>=3 and ell>=tau_D=3D/(sqrt(3D^2+4)+1). For D>=6, tau_D>3/2. Independently, for any finite source with D>=6, two-dimensional actual loaded hull, and a normalized nonnegative equilibrium stress, the loaded-only stress face lies on the full loaded-hull cycle; a nonunit side makes the face empty. This validates E.4 on the canonical D>=9 source. E.5 is the existing canonical unloaded-transversal theorem and was not assigned a duplicate ID. |
| Independent reconstruction | Derived the chord threshold from diameter-partner vectors, their common half-plane, the two diameter-disk tangent half-planes, unit separation of the opposite point, and the cosine rule. For loaded-only stresses, loaded near-unit adjacency puts every unit edge on the actual hull cycle; the virial identity makes the support nonempty, while the outward-normal equation rules out a leaf in any proper cycle subgraph. Thus the support is the full cycle. An occupied cap's opposite-side source point gives a nonunit closing chord, emptying the stress face; the canonical unloaded transversal then applies. |
| Dependencies and scope | The finite chord theorem is elementary and has no theorem dependencies. The stress-cycle extension uses only canonical C-LOADED-NEAR-UNIT-ADJACENCY; it needs no global minimality, triangle-free assumption, full-core hypothesis, or near-disk sequence. The E.5 consequence uses canonical C-UNLOADED-KKT-STRESS-TRANSVERSAL; it deletes at most s_Z+1 unloaded core vertices and retains all contact isolates. No packet/refill or same-cardinality result follows. |
| Counterexamples attempted | Checked coincident diameter partners, one vertical tangent side, collinear partner locations, all unit loaded edges versus the hull-cycle hypothesis, proper path subgraphs with leaves, and contact-isolated source labels. No counterexample survived the stated hypotheses. The cap arc budget was explicitly not used as a chord lower bound. |
| Verdict and canonical disposition | PASS — promote C-LOADED-EXTERIOR-CAP-CHORD-THRESHOLD; PASS WITH SCOPE EXTENSION — widen C-LOADED-ONLY-STRESS-CYCLE-OBSTRUCTION; PASS — E.4 application; PASS — E.5 already canonical under C-UNLOADED-KKT-STRESS-TRANSVERSAL. No result on refill or C-II closure. |
| Verification | Analytic audit only; no computational verifier claimed for the new bound. PR #783 remote `state-consistency` passed in 4m16s. |

The existing transversal then gives a strict descent after deleting at most s_Z+1 unloaded core points, but the result loses cardinality and supplies no refill or packet capacity. The Worker 2 frontier narrows to full-source restoration or an independent cardinality comparison; C-II and Level I remain open. |

| Independent reconstruction | Translate each packet along its strict separating unit normal to make all packet-to-retained lower constraints strict; internal distances remain unchanged and cross-packet constraints have strict margin. Then dilate each packet slightly to open every internal unit contact. Finite margins preserve all strict cross-packet and upper constraints. The final packet has no new unit contacts and remains triangle-free with the retained source; a removed old contact contradicts contact minimality if the diameter stays `D`, while covering all old diameter pairs makes every retained and inserted pair strictly shorter than `D`. |
| Dependencies and scope | No nontrivial theorem dependency. The proof retains all labels outside the deletion set and does not require actual-cap geometry, stress normalization, or the secondary diameter-pair minimum. It assumes strict cross-packet lower margins and strict upper slack on every packet-involving pair. |
| Counterexamples attempted | Checked a single packet with internal unit contacts and triangles, multiple packets with cross contacts, possible retained unit contacts, deletion of the last diameter pair, and the role of strict upper margins. The cross-contact and upper-bound edge cases are explicitly excluded by hypotheses; no counterexample remained. |
| Verdict and canonical disposition | PASS — promote `C-SEPARATED-PACKET-RELEASE-OBSTRUCTION`. It is complementary to the more general contact-count endpoint under a stronger source normalization, not a duplicate. |
| Verification | Independent analytic reconstruction; no finite verifier or computational certificate claimed. Source report SHA-256 rechecked at the immutable source commit. PR #785 remote state-consistency passed in 2m34s. |
| Frontier effect | Excludes only finite replacements satisfying the packet-separation and strict-margin hypotheses. It constructs no packet or spare capacity and does not change the C-II or Level I endpoint. |

| Field | Record |

| Exact claims audited | Finite all-source deficit-weighted cubic budget for inverse-D pairs, its global truncation, square-root matching for every linear near-diameter subgraph, the stronger distinct-partner bound for one-pair-per-owner packets, the separate bounded-degree near-unit matching fact, and the all-vertex near-diameter phase-purity estimate. An explicit nonglobal family has a linear inactive near-diameter star but actual diameter degree one. |
| Independent reconstruction | A maximal diameter-D convex completion has every boundary point incident to a diameter, and the planar constant-width characterization gives constant width D. Its curvature measure has density in [0,D], and the support-area quadratic form is positive semidefinite on antiperiodic H1. Near-diameter neighbors have support slack at most c/D; sublinearity controls the full angular interval, unit separation gives span at least (d-1)/(2D), and radius-2sqrt(c) support caps have overlap at most M_c including both antipodal lifts. The cutoff cosine test pays the nonzero curvature error; heavy vertices contribute at least D(tan(a)-a). Summing their interval functions, using overlap and the area-deficit Cauchy bound, yields the cubic moment with constant 3072M_c. Holder on a maximum-matching endpoint cover then gives the stated packet constant. The one-vertex version gives phase purity. |

A nonzero selected cut therefore separates zero from one owner when its region labels are constant. For pairwise disjoint proper cuts, this gives disjoint owner banks T_i. Each loaded-hull run maps to two distinct outer terminal occurrences in its own bank, while each rhombus-only root has its opposite moving/fixed contacts in one train of its root owner's bank. Summing disjoint owner banks proves sum_{i in I} r_i + |J minus I| <= T_OO. |
| Dependencies and scope | C-OO-LOWER-BLOCKER-LOCALIZATION, C-ACUTE-BINARY-OO-LAMINARITY, C-OWNER-REGION-TREE-RECURRENCE, C-NEAR-UNIT-CONFLICT-GEOMETRY, and C-LINEAR-NONMATCH. Pairwise disjoint proper crosscuts and a nonempty outer-walk complement for I are explicit hypotheses. Arbitrary crossing multishears, unloaded tails, contact-isolate endpoints, full-source feasibility, and global optimality are not included. |
| Counterexamples and corrections checked | Repeated labels on disconnected regions do not collide because each zero/owner cut has one label bank. Singleton bridges retain two distinct facial occurrences. I intersect J is charged once through r_i. The source verifier's abstract one-train unary packet has no source realization and does not refute this theorem. |
| Verdict and canonical disposition | PASS WITH NARROWED SCOPE — promote C-OO-OVERLAP-AWARE-ROOT-RUN-CAPACITY. The theorem closes Audit.63's owner-bank allocation gap only in the stated pairwise-disjoint acute-binary cut system. |

Mantel applies to actual track crossing graphs, not collapsed phases. No `q/D->pi` or `m=o(D)` assumption is used. |

| Exact claims audited | General maximal completion and Delaunay defect budget for a convex polygon with cyclic unit sides and a pairwise unit-separated union of wall vertices and strict-interior points; plus the one-deficit alternative under `0<=sigma0<=sqrt(3)/2+1/1000`. |
| Independent reconstruction | Oler gives nonnegative initial slack and bounds the number of auxiliary interior additions. A dense greedy extension is saturated in the plane without changing the maximal completion inside the wall. Each unit wall edge is a strict Gabriel/Delaunay edge, so the wall bounds a triangulated disk with exactly `2(z+k)+b-2` faces. Each face has circumradius at most one and sides at least one, giving area at least `sqrt(3)/4`; triangle excesses sum exactly to residual slack. Every right/obtuse face pairs across its longest internal side with a Delaunay neighbor and consumes at least `delta=(2-sqrt(3))/4` of two-face excess. Large-excess faces and non-six-valence interior vertices are charged to this same nonoverlapping excess, yielding `H<=4 sigma/delta` and at most `(450+12/delta)sigma` exceptional vertices. The one-deficit cap yields 51 right/obtuse faces, 137 defective faces, and 541 exceptional vertices; if one point is added, the canonical low-slack chart applies. |
| Hostile checks | The full boundary and interior point set is retained; auxiliary points remain explicitly non-source labels. The local near-equilateral estimates were checked at the actual `1/100` face-excess cutoff, not borrowed beyond the `1/1000` global chart theorem. The audited diffuse-strain regression is compatible: combinatorial defect bounds do not localize total strain, identify an exact vacancy, or imply global optimality. The bounded inactive-face `O(D)` use remains conditional and supplies no source move. |
| Verifier boundary | No verifier encodes the analytic defect theorem. Reproduced `research/verify_target_c_one_deficit_diffuse_strain_2026_10_07.py` (SHA-256 `ec04da709f68efd21630f045b8a020a223a55feb2af5c836fef3b5a6518ba324`) from the frozen snapshot; it passed 120, 300, 2,016, and 7,260 pair checks for `M=3,4,7,10`. - Independent proof: all active packet-to-wall directions lie in one closed angular interval of width `T+pi/3<=pi`. Translation along its bisector opens contacts because `|u+tn|^2=1+2t(n dot u)+t^2>1`, including equality at `T=2*pi/3`; a small packet dilation then opens all internal contacts. Releasing any weak packet into the canonical strict region yields exact weak capacity `|S_Q|`. The full-source exchange conclusion is limited to deletions that improve a canonical contact/diameter count or cover all old diameter pairs; neutral isolate replacements are not ruled out.
- Regression audit: exact enumeration found precisely three wall contacts at three distinct packet vertices. Their inward normals `(264,-23)/265`, `(-264,-23)/265`, `(0,1)` strictly surround zero and balance with positive weights `1,1,46/265`. Every moving vertex has positive pinned equilibrium, while no nonzero common packet translation is first-order feasible. Exact nonlinear release exists; the fixture is nonglobal and has a smaller 34-by-33 grid competitor.
- Verification: inspected and reran the Fraction / `Q(sqrt(3))` verifier at the frozen snapshot; it passed 1,128 old/endpoint pair checks for the ten-site shallow fixture and 600,060 old plus 600,060 endpoint checks for the 529-site regression. A separate exact rational enumeration confirmed one old diameter pair and the three wall-contact vectors. The script certifies fixtures only, not the analytic theorem or globality.
- Frontier effect: fixed-wall all-guard weak-packet surplus is eliminated only for a canonical cell whose wall turn is at most `2*pi/3`. No such turn bound or packet extraction is proved for arbitrary canonical sources; no source move, C-II closure, or Level I consequence follows.

Since each rhombus is one edge of the simple triangle-free track-crossing graph and deleting a short track removes at most its degree `d_tau`, at least `R4-epsilon*D^2` crossings remain among tracks with `d_tau>=L`. Mantel gives at least `2*sqrt(r-epsilon)*D-o(D)` such tracks; the canonical one-third bank bound gives `a>=a_star*D-o(D)` bank trains. Apply CB11 and divide by `D^2` along a subsequence realizing `x=liminf k/D`. If `x` is unbounded the claim is immediate. Otherwise `(a-5k)_+(L+1)<=80k^2/pi^2+40k/pi` implies `ell*(a_star-5x)_+<=80x^2/pi^2`. The positive root is the displayed constant; it is below `a_star/5`, so the same lower bound holds when `x>=a_star/5`. Endpoint-inclusive Menger gives `k` vertex-disjoint paths from distinct selected starts to distinct loaded vertices. |
| Hostile checks | The threshold is fixed before taking the sequence limit; `L+1>=ell*D`. The one-third bank is the exact finite bank theorem, not an independent-set inference from triangle-freeness. The crossing graph is the actual physical train graph, not an owner graph. If a selected terminal lies in the loaded set, its Menger path can be zero-length; the canonical consequence has been narrowed to say so explicitly. No path-length or finite-move conclusion follows. |
| Verifier boundary | The frozen exact `Fraction` verifier was re-inspected and rerun with the CB11 audit: 10 nonglobal fixtures, 13,259 lower-pair checks, 164 physical rails, 506 selections, and 27 flow/min-cut checks. Its finite antecedent failures and weaker `pi>3` rational bounds are reported in cycle 186; it does not prove the asymptotic train-bank theorem, source optimality, KKT, or the analytic separator bounds. |
| Verdict and canonical disposition | PASS — the fixed-`epsilon` CB13 separator lower bound survives independently. Clarify the Menger consequence in theorem state: the paths may be zero-length when a chosen start already belongs to the loaded set. This does not change the separator estimate or the frontier. |
| Frontier effect | The canonical stack now states only endpoint-disjoint reachability for the linear bank; it does not guarantee nontrivial return edges, return length, a useful pin, source motion, strict sub-`D` competition, or C-II closure. |

## Audit cycle 197 — second referee of Audit.174's loaded-hull measure premise

### Snapshot and question

- Refreshed `origin/main=c28830c6d319bd3c76b59e24bdb810aefcde0b16`, state `2026-10-09.audit.191`.
- Re-audited canonical `C-TYPEII-ATOMIC-SEPARATE-FACE-FLOOR` in [Audit.174](target_c_typeii_terminal_cancellation_face_floor_independent_audit_2026-10-08.md), focusing on the claim that the chosen loaded-hull side-normal measure is atomless. The near-disk theorem is stated for the full positive-contact-core hull, so I checked that this is the same hull used by the side measure.

### Independent reconstruction

Choose the canonical relative-interior KKT stress on the positive-contact core `G`, with `V=V(G)`, `B` the endpoints of `H_*`, and contacts `C_*`. Relative interior gives positive weight to every `C_*` contact; an unloaded `x in V` has no positive diameter multiplier, so equilibrium expresses it as a convex combination of its positive-contact neighbors. For every linear functional `u`, if its maximum on `V` is attained at an unloaded vertex, positivity of those contact weights forces all its neighbors to attain the same maximum. Connectedness propagates the maximum to a loaded vertex. Hence `max_V u=max_B u` for every `u`, so `conv(V)=conv(B)`. Contact-isolated source points are outside this core-hull identity and remain in the full source.

Consequently the Audit.174 loaded hull is the actual force hull in `C-FULLCORE-NEAR-DISK`, not a scaffold or a smaller endpoint hull. Its diameter-scaled perimeter-normal measure converges to the uniform perimeter-normal measure of the limiting disk, which is atomless. Each chosen side has length at least one, and different boundary components choose different sides; therefore the normalized chosen-side counting measure is dominated by that perimeter-weighted measure. This validates the atomless marginal used in the countably-supported-normal contradiction.

I also recomputed the separate-face incidence algebra: `N_partial >= q+f_partial-c_partial` and `a_partial=b+4f_partial+Q-Q_circle-2N_partial`, together with `b+Q=2(q-k)` and `Q_circle>=f-f_partial`, give `3 f_partial >= f+2k-2c_partial`. Adding the exact interior-only atomic-end count then gives `3(|A_D|+N_circle)<=5(f-k+c_partial)`. No occurrence or isolate deletion is needed.

### Verification and verdict

The frozen exact checker was rerun at this main snapshot: PASS, with 7,252 source lower-pair checks, 108 physical terminal-pair checks, 620 component identities, and 886 separate-face cancellation checks across eight nonglobal fixtures. These checks remain finite identity tests; they do not certify the weak-limit, atom-selection, or global-source arguments.

**PASS — no defect found in the loaded-hull measure bridge or the separate-face incidence algebra.** This is a targeted second-referee check of an already canonical theorem, not a new theorem or frontier promotion. State remains `.191`; C-II remains open. No discovery branch or source worktree was modified.

- Audit base: `origin/main=779e26d0a6d132584d68e4f7c0604ed5d17575e6`, state `2026-10-09.audit.194`.

- Local-capacity source: `docs/reports/target_c_localized_curved_boundary_slow_lattice_deformation_2026-10-09.md`, SHA-256 `2bc5da1876e0b2cd079fe2c533b62c8d884a61d845a90cb652dcfbcf28cf5a48`.
- The source's separate global face-tail, strain, and scalar claims were not audited in this cycle.

| Field | Audit record |
| --- | --- |
| Exact claims | (i) At most a fixed number `J` of unit-rhombic lattice charts with `K/D`-Lipschitz displacement contain at most `C_{J,K}D^(4/5)` actual diameter endpoints apart from uncovered endpoints. (ii) For each fixed `K`, no unbounded canonical Type-II sequence has a nonempty unloaded positive-contact cell whose every internal actual contact and every contact to its own loaded attachments is within `K/D` of one unit triangular six-direction frame. Frames may vary by source; other cells and loaded-loaded contacts are unrestricted. |
| Dependencies | The generic count in claim (i) has no nontrivial dependency; its canonical C-II contradiction corollary uses `C-ZRF` for `h>=ceil(D)` and `C-WOODALL` for `h<=q`. For claim (ii), canonical `C-UNIFORM-HARMONIC-CELL-AREA` gives `|A_I|>=cD/3` for fixed `c<sin(pi/12)/2`; canonical `C-TYPEII-FACE-TAIL-PORT-MULTIPLICITY` gives an `eta D` exceptional incidence bound with actual routes of length `<lambda D` to every nearby cell vertex. No global face-tail limit or zero holonomy is used. |
| Independent reconstruction | The radius-`D` ball hull contains a radius-`c_*D` disk and all actual diameter endpoints lie on its boundary. For a patch `E=W intersect B(c,r)`, uniformly conditioned unit-rhombic-lattice points within `epsilon` of `partial E` are bounded by `C(r epsilon+(Dr)^(1/3)+1)`: mollified Poisson summation on the outer parallel body and inner erosion has error `O(sqrt(D/s))`; their area gap is `O(r(epsilon+s)+1)`; choose `s=(D/r^2)^(1/3)`. For claim (i), cover `conv(B_D)` by `O(D/r)` patches with `r=D^(4/5)`; each Lipschitz chart has physical error at most `2Kr/D`, giving the exponent `4/5` after summing. For claim (ii), use `r=rho D/2`. Patches with no good port account for `<eta D` attachments. In every other patch, route separately from a good anchor to one internal neighbor of each attachment, append its actual attachment edge, and sum frame-rounding errors to `K(lambda+1/D)`. The local count gives `limsup |A_I|/D<=eta+C K lambda`, contradicting `|A_I|/D>=c/3` after choosing `eta,lambda` small. No path-label consistency is needed. |
| Hostile scope | The phase hypothesis covers every actual internal and attachment contact of the whole cell. It is not supplied by a selected complete grid or a coherent subgrain. The theorem says nothing about other cells, loaded-loaded contacts, or global source phase. Contact isolates remain in the full source. |
| Verifier | No finite verifier certifies the local curvature/Fourier estimate; none is claimed. The registered finite certificates for the canonical route theorem certify only their encoded finite subclaims. |
| Verdict | PASS — promote `C-SLOW-LATTICE-DIAMETER-ENDPOINT-BOUND` with no nontrivial dependencies, and `C-TYPEII-SINGLE-CELL-INVERSE-PHASE-EXCLUSION` with dependencies `C-UNIFORM-HARMONIC-CELL-AREA` and `C-TYPEII-FACE-TAIL-PORT-MULTIPLICITY`. |
| Frontier effect | Excludes all-but-`o(D)` endpoint covers by a fixed number of slowly deformed unit-rhombic charts, and excludes one whole-cell inverse-phase premise without a global face-tail limit or zero holonomy. No chart cover is extracted, no violating-contact count or motion/payment follows, and C-II/Level I remain open. |
| Promotion transaction | The theorem-bearing commit `5fd219ed9393ff9ea9fad93d80e4bb36a1d8b5b0` was merged by PR #901 as `3fee7c922dc5e6c1ec766ef8898c13105c8dd0de`; protected `state-consistency` run `37954390846` passed, including registered certificates. |

## Audit cycle 202 — global inverse-phase and vanishing face-tail exclusion

### Frozen candidate and authority

- Audit base: `origin/main=3fee7c922dc5e6c1ec766ef8898c13105c8dd0de`, state `2026-10-09.audit.195`.
- Frozen source: `origin/research/level-i-fused-review-20261009` at `6132201c1d3056ca68a222217094acb819f5b4d7`; unchanged at post-audit refresh.
- Candidate artifact: `docs/reports/target_c_global_inverse_phase_face_tail_exclusion_2026-10-09.md`, SHA-256 `2a2c12e3b63e9b0295e1106eda89b68449ed1b37a14d114a8e24d6e5f7e73bcd`.
- Section 8's separate strain claim was audited in Audit.203; Section 9 onward remains unreviewed.

| Field | Audit record |
| --- | --- |
| Exact claim | In an unbounded canonical post-ZF C-II sequence, if every actual contact of the connected positive-contact core lies within fixed `K/D` of one unit triangular frame and the tail `T_M` of actual hull-augmented face-path lengths satisfies `lim_{M->infinity} limsup_D T_M/D=0`, then the loaded endpoint population is `q=o(D)`. This contradicts `h>=ceil(D)` and `h<=q`. |
| Dependencies | `C-RHOMBUS-POP` supplies connectedness, `F=conv(B)=conv(V)`, the convex hull augmentation, and the old-face/pocket structure. Each actual diameter endpoint is exposed in the full-source hull by its actual diameter partner, so membership in `V` follows from `F=conv(B)=conv(V)`; `C-GENERIC-ISOLATE-ANCHOR-AVOIDANCE` is not used. `C-SLOW-LATTICE-DIAMETER-ENDPOINT-BOUND` supplies the curved-boundary chart count. `C-ZRF` gives `h>=ceil(D)`; `C-WOODALL` gives `h<=q`. |
| Independent reconstruction | The iterated tail limit implies the maximum old bounded contact-face length is `o(D)`. Nearest-frame labels therefore sum to zero on every old bounded face; pockets are not contact cycles, and the old facial cycles generate the cycle space of `G`, yielding a global lattice potential `ell`. A two-segment generic arc routed through convex faces gives `d_G(p,q)<=C_M(|p-q|+1)+2T_M`. Fix `M` with `limsup T_M/D<1`; unit separation absorbs the additive tail and yields a `K'/D`-Lipschitz displacement on `V`. For large `D`, the lattice potential is injective; converting the bound to lattice coordinates gives one `2K'/D`-Lipschitz chart on all endpoints. Audit.201 then gives `q=O(D^(4/5))`, contradicting the canonical lower count. Strict support plus `F=conv(B)=conv(V)` places every endpoint in `V`. |
| Hostile scope | The global phase is required on every actual core contact, including those on exceptional faces. Selected trains or grids do not suffice. Routes use only actual contacts; auxiliary hull sides only define the planar faces. Contact isolates remain in the source and in the ball-hull geometry. The tail and phase premises are not extracted. |
| Verifier | No finite verifier certifies the analytic curved-boundary lattice estimate; Audit.201 makes no such claim. The new face routing and limit deductions are reconstructed directly. |
| Verdict | PASS WITH NARROWED SCOPE — promote `C-GLOBAL-INVERSE-PHASE-FACE-TAIL-EXCLUSION` under both explicit premises. |
| Frontier effect | None: the two joint premises remain unavailable, so no C-II branch is eliminated and Level I remains open. |
| Promotion transaction | PR #902 head `39df29d40aa6d7ca3e8babbf59d11211e399b1bf` merged as `2b98ec79a1b219d9476a7a879e0aaacd8bc8fcf4`; protected `state-consistency` run `37957756911` passed, including all registered certificates. |

| Field | Audit record |
| --- | --- |
| Exact claim | For any distinct planar anchors `a_0,a_1,a_2` and integers `0<r<s`, the unit directions `t` for which some translation `z` satisfies `|z-a_0|=|z+r*t-a_1|=|z+s*t-a_2|=1` form a set of size at most ten. Several translations at one fixed direction are allowed. |

| Independent reconstruction | Subtracting squared equations gives a two-by-two linear system in `z` with determinant `d(t)=det(t,sA-rB)+det(A,B)`. If it vanishes identically, `A/r=B/s=c` and the equations force `t=c`, so at most one direction occurs. Otherwise there are at most two singular directions. For all others, Cramer's rule makes `z(t)` rational with affine denominator and quadratic numerators; `|z|^2=1` gives a degree-four polynomial on the circle and at most eight regular roots unless it is an identity. In the identity case, a linear combination makes one coordinate of `z(t)` affine `L(t)`. Rational-square parity at both level sets `L=+1` and `L=-1` forces both lines tangent, hence `L` is a unit linear coordinate and `z(t)=Ot` for an orthogonal `O`. Constant `z`, nontrivial reflections, invertible rotations, and the sole singular rotation `r=1,O=-I` each contradict distinct anchors or `r<s`. Thus the identity case is impossible, leaving at most eight regular plus two singular directions. |
| Hostile scope and regressions | Singular directions are counted even when they admit many translations. Distinctness of all three anchors is used to make `A,B,A-B` nonzero. The separately stated repeated-anchor variant is not promoted and retains its explicit additional cross-pair lower bound. The exact theorem implies no source chain exists and no finite motion. |
| Verifier boundary | No finite verifier is claimed. The proof uses exact algebra, complex intersection multiplicities for the rational-square identity, and a degree-four circle-root count; it does not rely on numerical root finding. |

### Independent reconstruction and validation boundary

The promoted claim uses canonical C-QUARTER-FOLD-CONVEX-FACE-CLASSIFICATION for the four transitions and their parallelogram. If one transition arc has one edge, equality of opposite parallelogram chords forces the opposite arc to be one edge too; otherwise a unit chord would lie inside the actual face. On a nonquadrilateral face the other two arcs have internal vertices. At the two ends of one long arc, full-source lower feasibility at both the original and folded endpoints puts each old transition turn strictly between pi/3 and 2pi/3. With a=0, b=Lt, d=v, c=Lt+v, the first long-arc edges are e=(cos alpha)t-(sin alpha)Jt and f=(cos beta)t+(sin beta)Jt; the turn bounds imply pi/3+delta<gamma-d0<2pi/3-delta for delta=(alpha+beta)/2, d0=(beta-alpha)/2. Undoing the common quarter-turn translates one long arc by -2v, giving the new two-point squared distance 1+4sin^2(delta)-4sin(delta)sin(gamma-d0)<1 if delta>0. If delta=0, an old nonconsecutive unit chord results. Therefore no transition arc has one edge, so a nonconstant pattern needs at least eight sides and at eight all four arcs have length two.

The exact rational eight-cycle sharpness example was checked independently using integer coordinates scaled by 41: all 28 pair checks, eight cycle contacts, zero signed closure, exact quarter-fold edge increments, no endpoint triangle, and endpoint minimum noncontact squared distance 1922/1681. This checks the stated finite example only. Audit.83's regular-hexagon collision and rectangle-with-interior-chord regressions were checked against the scope; neither is a counterexample.

## Audit cycle 225 — guarded parallelogram and eight-face isolate cap

### Snapshot

- Canonical base: `origin/main=31c71068c57ea39e83f378ed7a417fc707e97ba5`, state `2026-10-09.audit.217`; the audit branch was rebased onto `0e815125bbdbc88b28884b9d44ad5ea9b08024bb` after PR #932 merged, with the theorem dependencies unchanged.

- Independent audit: `docs/audits/target_c_quarter_fold_eight_face_isolate_bound_independent_audit_2026-10-09.md`.

| Field | Audit record |
| --- | --- |
| Exact claims promoted | (1) For any nondegenerate parallelogram with side lengths at most `sqrt(3)`, its weak unit-clearance set is contained in the closed radius-`1/2` disk about the center, with diameter at most one; its strict-clearance set lies in the open disk and has diameter strictly below one. (2) An actual bounded convex eight-sided unit-contact face admitting a nonconstant face-closed fold with a full unit-separated triangle-free endpoint contains at most one contact-isolated source point in its interior. |
| Dependencies | Claim (1) has none. Claim (2) uses `C-QUARTER-FOLD-CONVEX-FACE-CLASSIFICATION`, `C-QUARTER-FOLD-MINIMUM-FACE-SIZE`, `C-QUARTER-FOLD-EIGHT-FACE-AREA-LOWER-BOUND`, and Claim (1). |
| Independent reconstruction | Expand the parallelogram side Gram matrix to equal diagonals `3`, producing a distance-expanding map to a rhombus of side `sqrt(3)`. In rhombus coordinates `(+-A,0),(0,+-B)`, `A^2+B^2=3`. If a half-diagonal exceeds `3/2`, two circumradius-`3/(2A)` triangles cover the rhombus strictly. Otherwise the product `A^2B^2>=27/16`; first-quadrant weak-clear points have `chi,psi<1/2`, a four-vertex variance identity gives `t=x^2+y^2<=1/2`, and the two nearest guards sharpen this to `t<=1-sqrt(A^2B^2/3)<=1/4`. Equality in the product bound forces the strict-clearance set empty; otherwise the radius is uniformly below `1/2`. In an eligible eight-face, opposite arc midpoints form a nondegenerate central parallelogram whose sides are the two-step chords across transition corners; old and folded lower feasibility put each transition turn in `(pi/3,2pi/3)`, so all middle-parallelogram sides are below `sqrt(3)`. Four old corner triangles have unit legs and circumradius below one, excluding every contact isolate from them. All remaining isolates lie strictly clear of the middle parallelogram's four vertices and hence in one open radius-`1/2` disk, so there can be at most one. |
| Verifier boundary and scope | No script encodes the analytic parallelogram optimization or face partition. Audit.222's exact 8-cycle contact check and the Section 5.5 isolate distances are consistent with the cap; the rational nine-site example has exactly one isolate. The theorem counts only isolates in one stated bounded face and gives no bound on the number of active faces or on outside capacity. |
| Hostile edge case | At `A^2B^2=27/16`, the weak-clearance set can contain opposite points at distance exactly one, so the weak diameter cannot be sharpened. Those points are not strictly clear; the strict set is empty. Merely putting strict points in an open radius-`1/2` disk would not alone establish a strict bound on its supremum diameter, so the equality case was checked separately. |
| Verdict | PASS — promote `C-PARALLELOGRAM-UNIT-GUARD-CENTER-BOUND` and `C-QUARTER-FOLD-EIGHT-FACE-ISOLATE-BOUND`. |
| Frontier effect | None. Each active eligible eight-face holds at most one old contact isolate, and the general guard lemma controls that isolate locally; this gives no source-wide face count or relocation/payment theorem. C-II and Level I remain open. |
| Promotion transaction | PR #933 head `09c94699b508af217befe6dfc045d2c38ea13bfe` merged as `cda3e4ec41292c58875ac918ccd7977bbd89ea20`. | Independent reconstruction | Opposite transition arcs have equal chords. A one-edge arc forces its opposite to be one edge too, since an equal unit chord spanning a longer old face arc would split the actual face. If a one-edge pair exists on an `m>=5` face, the other pair of opposite arcs is long. Let the short sides be the unit vector `v` and long chord direction `t`; convexity writes the first long-arc edges as `e=cos(alpha)t-sin(alpha)Jt` and `f=cos(beta)t+sin(beta)Jt`. Old actual-face and folded triangle-free chord bounds put the two transition turns in `(pi/3,2pi/3)`. The turns at adjacent corners force `pi/3+beta<gamma<2pi/3-alpha`; with `delta=(alpha+beta)/2`, `d0=(beta-alpha)/2`, this gives `pi/3+delta<gamma-d0<2pi/3-delta`. The folded first internal labels then have squared distance `1+4sin^2(delta)-4sin(delta)sin(gamma-d0)<1` if `delta>0`. If `delta=0`, their old distance is the unit vector `v` between nonconsecutive actual face vertices, also impossible. |
| Hostile scope and verifier boundary | The four-side quadrilateral exception is outside `m>=5`. Re-enumerating the rational eight-cycle after scaling by 41 gives positive old turn cross-products (minimum 720), exactly eight unit contacts and no triangles in both lists, minimum noncontact squared distances `4802/1681` and `1922/1681`, zero signed edge sum, and exact folded increments. The folded polygon is not assumed convex. Thus the local finite example makes eight sharp; it is nonglobal. No verifier encodes the universal angle argument; this arithmetic is only a finite sharpness check. |

## Audit cycle 243 — explicit asymptotic coefficient for the global population reserve

### Snapshot and claim

- Canonical base: `origin/main=1c12b5dcbbc5cc9b0cc9ffd697d77f9bfffe01d8`, state `2026-10-10.audit.231`.
- Frozen candidate: `origin/research/level-i-fused-review-20261009` at `6132201c1d3056ca68a222217094acb819f5b4d7`; source report SHA-256 `a8e0334ad495eb5e4eefda1d1b4a1a5a1bf3a14e5851ef46e99cd4d2bfca70a2`.
- Claim: for every fixed `theta` in `(131/208,2/3)`, each sufficiently large global unit-separated minimizer obeys `g_A>=kappa0*D^(2/3)-C_theta*D^theta`, with `kappa0=(3*pi/(5*a0))*(3*a0/16)^(2/3)` and `a0=sqrt(3)/2`; consequently `liminf g_A/D^(2/3)>=kappa0`.
- Independent source check: Huxley's Theorem A in the author’s Oberwolfach Report 14/2008, pp. 703–704, gives `N(r,P)=A*r^2+O(r^(131/208+epsilon))` under `C^3` smoothness. The full 2003 article was not inspected; only the fixed centered disk instance is used.

### Verdict and disposition

**PASS — promote `C-GLOBAL-POPULATION-EXCESS-ASYMPTOTIC-COEFFICIENT`.** The Stieltjes error is controlled by the supremum disk discrepancy times the total variation `L_S(D)=O_c(1)` of the shrinking-interval lens weight; the lower endpoint uses the left limit so exact distance-`D` conflicts are deleted. The lens expansion gives conflict coefficient `32*pi/(15*a0^2)*c^(5/2)`, whose difference from the population gain `pi/a0*c` is maximized at `c=(3*a0/16)^(2/3)`. One endpoint per conflict edge leaves a strict-sub-`D` lattice set; the global-minimizer integer comparison gives `n-1` without rounding loss.

The theorem sharpens the coefficient in Audit.199 and has no internal theorem dependency. The source report’s separate triangle-free deformation and one-lattice shell claims are not included. No translation-uniform oval bound, source motion, triangle conclusion, C-II closure, or Level I result is claimed. The active C-II payment obligation is unchanged. No theorem-specific verifier exists; the source branch’s two-port finite checker is unrelated.

### Validation

The MFO primary transcription was downloaded and its printed pages 703–704 inspected. This is an analytic proof with no finite certificate. The theorem-state transaction, generated views, route summaries, registered certificate suite, and `git diff --check` are recorded with the promotion commit.

## Audit cycle 249 — second referee of midpoint-tail band counting

- **Verdict:** PASS — corroborate Audit.246's midpoint-tail, actual-diameter-edge transfer, and near-diameter localization. No counterexample or scope error found.
- **Proof clarification:** the dyadic derivative-band count should specify angular windows of width `a/(2D)` (at most `1/2` since `a<D` for `D>=216`). The normal-window estimate gives `O(a+1)` selected endpoints per window; total windows are `O(D Lambda/a+N_c)`, where `Lambda<=4E/a^2` and `N_c<=8DE/a^3`. Thus `N_band=O(DE/a^2+(a+1)DE/a^3)=O(D^2/a^2)`. This supplies the explicit missing subdivision scale behind (17.4); it does not change the theorem statement or dependencies.
- The edge-midpoint result still uses an injective endpoint assignment from the full canonical Woodall graph. The near-diameter estimate remains unweighted and makes no pair-count claim. All source labels, including contact isolates, are retained. No finite verifier encodes the analytic proof.
- **Disposition:** update the Audit.246 proof prose only; no theorem-state bump, dependency change, route/prompt change, or frontier effect. The .235 promotion remains on PR #947 and is not yet canonical on main.
